## Q1-r1

arm `folder`, model `claude-haiku-5-5`, effort `low`, wall 10.2 s

**Prompt** (from the .meta.json):

```
You are answering an actuarial question about Indiana's dig-damage enforcement record: the Indiana
Utility Regulatory Commission's orders approving the Underground Plant Protection Advisory Committee's
recommendations under Indiana Code chapter 8-1-26, for the 58 orders dated 2021-01-01 through 2025-12-31.
Definitions used in every question:
- **Case:** one recommendation form in an order (one case number on one page).
- **Year:** the year of the order's date, not the damage date, unless the question says otherwise.
- **Cite:** the IC 8-1-26 citation under "finds the following violation(s)". Every case has exactly one.
  **Base section:** the cite without "IC 8-1-26-" and without any parenthesized word. Example:
  "IC 8-1-26-16(h)(expire)" has base section "16(h)" and qualifier "expire". A parenthesized word is
  two or more letters (hyphens allowed); single letters or digits in parentheses, like (h) or (a)(2), are
  part of the base section, so "20(a)(3)(b)" is its own base section, not "20(a)(2)".
- **Headline penalty:** the first dollar amount in the committee's recommendation, read as printed but
  ignoring a doubled "$" and any space after a thousands comma ("$$1000" is $1,000; "$3, 500" is $3,500).
  A recommendation with no "$" amount has a headline penalty of $0.
- **Utility respondent:** a case whose respondent name, ignoring case and leading spaces, starts with one of:
  "CenterPoint", "Northern Indiana Public Service", "NIPSCO", "Citizens Gas", "Citizens Energy",
  "Vectren", "Indiana American Water", "Ohio Valley Gas", "American Electric Power", "Duke Energy",
  "Community Natural Gas", "Midwest Natural Gas", "Frontier Communications". Every other case is a
  **non-utility respondent**.
Give each number exactly as the question asks (rounding stated). If you cannot compute it from data you
have, say so and give your best estimate, labelled as an estimate.

Frequency: across all five years, what percentage of cases have base section 16(h), and what percentage have base section 20(a)(2)? Round to one decimal.
```

```
You are a careful analyst. You can use the Read, Grep, Glob and Bash tools (Bash has Python 3). Your working directory is the only place you may look for files; use only files in it. Show the number or answer each question asks for in your final reply.
```

**Final answer** (verbatim):

```
**Answers (all 58 orders, 2021-01-01 to 2025-12-31, 9,958 cases):**

- **Base section 16(h): 3,941 cases, 39.6%**
- **Base section 20(a)(2): 2,284 cases, 22.9%**

Both figures are the same whether or not the one case with a blank cite is counted in the denominator (9,957 gives 39.6% and 22.9%).

Notes:
- I worked from the raw `cases.csv`. The schema also describes a `cases_clean.csv` with corrections applied, but that file isn't in the folder, so the figures are not corrected for the known defects.
- The file has 9,958 rows from 58 orders, all dated within the window, so no rows were dropped for dates.
- The one row with a blank cite is the page with case number `0` (ord_PSD_020321.pdf, page 27), which the schema says to treat as unusable. It is included in the denominator here.
- Cites were reduced to base sections by stripping `IC 8-1-26-` and any parenthesized word of two or more letters. Single-letter and digit parentheticals like (h) and (a)(2) were kept, so 20(a)(2) and 20(a)(3)(b) are separate sections.
```

```
You are answering an actuarial question about Indiana's dig-damage enforcement record: the Indiana
Utility Regulatory Commission's orders approving the Underground Plant Protection Advisory Committee's
recommendations under Indiana Code chapter 8-1-26, for the 58 orders dated 2021-01-01 through 2025-12-31.
Definitions used in every question:
- **Case:** one recommendation form in an order (one case number on one page).
- **Year:** the year of the order's date, not the damage date, unless the question says otherwise.
- **Cite:** the IC 8-1-26 citation under "finds the following violation(s)". Every case has exactly one.
  **Base section:** the cite without "IC 8-1-26-" and without any parenthesized word. Example:
  "IC 8-1-26-16(h)(expire)" has base section "16(h)" and qualifier "expire". A parenthesized word is
  two or more letters (hyphens allowed); single letters or digits in parentheses, like (h) or (a)(2), are
  part of the base section, so "20(a)(3)(b)" is its own base section, not "20(a)(2)".
- **Headline penalty:** the first dollar amount in the committee's recommendation, read as printed but
  ignoring a doubled "$" and any space after a thousands comma ("$$1000" is $1,000; "$3, 500" is $3,500).
  A recommendation with no "$" amount has a headline penalty of $0.
- **Utility respondent:** a case whose respondent name, ignoring case and leading spaces, starts with one of:
  "CenterPoint", "Northern Indiana Public Service", "NIPSCO", "Citizens Gas", "Citizens Energy",
  "Vectren", "Indiana American Water", "Ohio Valley Gas", "American Electric Power", "Duke Energy",
  "Community Natural Gas", "Midwest Natural Gas", "Frontier Communications". Every other case is a
  **non-utility respondent**.
Give each number exactly as the question asks (rounding stated). If you cannot compute it from data you
have, say so and give your best estimate, labelled as an estimate.
```

```
Trend: how many cases are there in each year 2021, 2022, 2023, 2024 and 2025, and which year has the most?
```

````
**Answer:** 0.0% of utility-respondent cases and 28.1% of non-utility-respondent cases have a recommendation containing "warning letter" (any capitalization).

- **Utility respondent:** 1 of 2,715 cases (0.0%; the exact share is 0.037%).
- **Non-utility respondent:** 2,032 of 7,243 cases (28.1%; the exact share is 28.05%).

**Reading taken:** "Warning letter" is matched as a case-insensitive substring of the recommendation text. Utility status uses the PREAMBLE's 13-prefix test. Counts are rows, one per case, across all 58 orders (9,958 rows in total). The denominators are the utility and non-utility rows, not all 9,958.

**Command and output** (run from `/tmp/r2run-wb5qnv8h/folder`):

```
python3 -I reference/tools/cases.py count --utility yes --rec-contains "warning letter"
  count = 1 of 9958 rows ... rows kept: 2715 denominator (utility yes: 2715 of 9958)

python3 -I reference/tools/cases.py count --utility no --rec-contains "warning letter"
  count = 2032 of 9958 rows (utility no: 7243 rows kept)
```

**Receipts:**
- Utility, the one match: `ord_PSD_113022.pdf` p175, case 27398, IC 8-1-26-20(a)(2), recommendation "Warning letter", respondent "Citizens Energy Group - Steam Division".
- Non-utility: 2,032 rows, with the same wording check run on the full data. The tool's `count --rec-contains "warning letter"` gives 2,033 across all rows, so 1 match is a utility row.

**Caveats:**
- The utility figure rests on a single case, so it is a count of 1, not a rate that is meaningful at this scale.
- The matching is a plain substring test. A recommendation that says "warning-letter" with a hyphen would not match. Searching for "warning" alone gives the same counts (1 utility, 2,032 non-utility), so the wording check changes nothing here.
````

```
recommendations under Indiana Code chapter 8-1-26, for the 58 orders dated 2021-01-01 through 2025-12-31.
Definitions used in every question:
- **Case:** one recommendation form in an order (one case number on one page).
- **Year:** the year of the order's date, not the damage date, unless the question says otherwise.
- **Cite:** the IC 8-1-26 citation under "finds the following violation(s)". Every case has exactly one.
  **Base section:** the cite without "IC 8-1-26-" and without any parenthesized word. Example:
  "IC 8-1-26-16(h)(expire)" has base section "16(h)" and qualifier "expire". A parenthesized word is
  two or more letters (hyphens allowed); single letters or digits in parentheses, like (h) or (a)(2), are
  part of the base section, so "20(a)(3)(b)" is its own base section, not "20(a)(2)".
- **Headline penalty:** the first dollar amount in the committee's recommendation, read as printed but
  ignoring a doubled "$" and any space after a thousands comma ("$$1000" is $1,000; "$3, 500" is $3,500).
  A recommendation with no "$" amount has a headline penalty of $0.
- **Utility respondent:** a case whose respondent name, ignoring case and leading spaces, starts with one of:
  "CenterPoint", "Northern Indiana Public Service", "NIPSCO", "Citizens Gas", "Citizens Energy",
  "Vectren", "Indiana American Water", "Ohio Valley Gas", "American Electric Power", "Duke Energy",
  "Community Natural Gas", "Midwest Natural Gas", "Frontier Communications". Every other case is a
  **non-utility respondent**.
Give each number exactly as the question asks (rounding stated). If you cannot compute it from data you
have, say so and give your best estimate, labelled as an estimate.

Warnings: what percentage of utility-respondent cases, and what percentage of non-utility-respondent cases, have a recommendation containing the words "warning letter" (any capitalization)? Round to one decimal.
```

```
You are answering an actuarial question about Indiana's dig-damage enforcement record: the Indiana
Utility Regulatory Commission's orders approving the Underground Plant Protection Advisory Committee's
recommendations under Indiana Code chapter 8-1-26, for the 58 orders dated 2021-01-01 through 2025-12-31.
Definitions used in every question:
- **Case:** one recommendation form in an order (one case number on one page).
- **Year:** the year of the order's date, not the damage date, unless the question says otherwise.
- **Cite:** the IC 8-1-26 citation under "finds the following violation(s)". Every case has exactly one.
  **Base section:** the cite without "IC 8-1-26-" and without any parenthesized word. Example:
  "IC 8-1-26-16(h)(expire)" has base section "16(h)" and qualifier "expire". A parenthesized word is
  two or more letters (hyphens allowed); single letters or digits in parentheses, like (h) or (a)(2), are
  part of the base section, so "20(a)(3)(b)" is its own base section, not "20(a)(2)".
- **Headline penalty:** the first dollar amount in the committee's recommendation, read as printed but
  ignoring a doubled "$" and any space after a thousands comma ("$$1000" is $1,000; "$3, 500" is $3,500).
  A recommendation with no "$" amount has a headline penalty of $0.
- **Utility respondent:** a case whose respondent name, ignoring case and leading spaces, starts with one of:
  "CenterPoint", "Northern Indiana Public Service", "NIPSCO", "Citizens Gas", "Citizens Energy",
  "Vectren", "Indiana American Water", "Ohio Valley Gas", "American Electric Power", "Duke Energy",
  "Community Natural Gas", "Midwest Natural Gas", "Frontier Communications". Every other case is a
  **non-utility respondent**.
Give each number exactly as the question asks (rounding stated). If you cannot compute it from data you
have, say so and give your best estimate, labelled as an estimate.
```

```
Training option: among cases with a headline penalty above $0, what percentage have a recommendation that contains the word "training" (any capitalization)? Round to one decimal.
```

**Final answer** (verbatim):

```
**Answer: 187 distinct normalized respondents have 5 or more non-utility cases. Together they account for 1,760 of 7,243 non-utility-respondent cases, or 24.3%.**

**Data and method**
- Source: `folder/reference/cases.csv`, 9,958 rows (one row per recommendation form, which is the question's definition of a case).
- I parsed the file with Python's csv module and applied the question's definitions directly. I did not use the folder's helper tool, since the question's definitions take precedence.
- Utility test: the respondent, with leading spaces removed and lowercased, starts with one of the 13 listed prefixes. This gives 2,715 utility cases and 7,243 non-utility cases.
- Normalization: lowercase, delete characters that are not letters, digits or spaces, collapse and trim spaces, then drop one final word if it is inc, llc, co, corp, corporation or company. This gives 4,727 distinct normalized non-utility respondents.

**Computation**
- Respondents with 5 or more cases: 187.
- Their cases: 1,760 of 7,243 non-utility cases = 24.3%.
- Largest groups: "miller pipeline" (91), "riethriley construction co" (54), "calumet civil contractors" (48), "milestone contractors lp" (46), "milenium" (36).

**Reading taken**
- Each row counts as one case, so the two repeated case numbers (25064 and 33669) are counted twice. This follows the question's definition. Counting them once would change the denominator by at most two and could change the percentage only marginally.
- The one blank respondent is non-utility and forms a group of 1, so it does not affect the 5-or-more count.

**Caveats**
- Spelling variants are not merged, as the question requires. For example, "milestone contractors lp" and "milestone contractors north" are counted separately, and "internattional" appears as a misspelling of "international". Merging variants would likely increase the number of groups at 5 or more and the concentration figure.
```

```
  two or more letters (hyphens allowed); single letters or digits in parentheses, like (h) or (a)(2), are
  part of the base section, so "20(a)(3)(b)" is its own base section, not "20(a)(2)".
- **Headline penalty:** the first dollar amount in the committee's recommendation, read as printed but
  ignoring a doubled "$" and any space after a thousands comma ("$$1000" is $1,000; "$3, 500" is $3,500).
  A recommendation with no "$" amount has a headline penalty of $0.
- **Utility respondent:** a case whose respondent name, ignoring case and leading spaces, starts with one of:
  "CenterPoint", "Northern Indiana Public Service", "NIPSCO", "Citizens Gas", "Citizens Energy",
  "Vectren", "Indiana American Water", "Ohio Valley Gas", "American Electric Power", "Duke Energy",
  "Community Natural Gas", "Midwest Natural Gas", "Frontier Communications". Every other case is a
  **non-utility respondent**.
```

```
def is_utility(respondent):
    """Respondent name, ignoring case and leading spaces, starts with one of UTILITY_PREFIXES."""
    s = (respondent or "").lstrip().casefold()
    return any(s.startswith(p.casefold()) for p in UTILITY_PREFIXES)


def order_year(row):
```

```
You are answering an actuarial question about Indiana's dig-damage enforcement record: the Indiana
Utility Regulatory Commission's orders approving the Underground Plant Protection Advisory Committee's
recommendations under Indiana Code chapter 8-1-26, for the 58 orders dated 2021-01-01 through 2025-12-31.
Definitions used in every question:
- **Case:** one recommendation form in an order (one case number on one page).
- **Year:** the year of the order's date, not the damage date, unless the question says otherwise.
- **Cite:** the IC 8-1-26 citation under "finds the following violation(s)". Every case has exactly one.
  **Base section:** the cite without "IC 8-1-26-" and without any parenthesized word. Example:
  "IC 8-1-26-16(h)(expire)" has base section "16(h)" and qualifier "expire". A parenthesized word is
  two or more letters (hyphens allowed); single letters or digits in parentheses, like (h) or (a)(2), are
  part of the base section, so "20(a)(3)(b)" is its own base section, not "20(a)(2)".
- **Headline penalty:** the first dollar amount in the committee's recommendation, read as printed but
  ignoring a doubled "$" and any space after a thousands comma ("$$1000" is $1,000; "$3, 500" is $3,500).
  A recommendation with no "$" amount has a headline penalty of $0.
- **Utility respondent:** a case whose respondent name, ignoring case and leading spaces, starts with one of:
  "CenterPoint", "Northern Indiana Public Service", "NIPSCO", "Citizens Gas", "Citizens Energy",
  "Vectren", "Indiana American Water", "Ohio Valley Gas", "American Electric Power", "Duke Energy",
  "Community Natural Gas", "Midwest Natural Gas", "Frontier Communications". Every other case is a
  **non-utility respondent**.
Give each number exactly as the question asks (rounding stated). If you cannot compute it from data you
have, say so and give your best estimate, labelled as an estimate.
```

```
Escalation: how many cases have a recommendation containing "ticket escalation" (any capitalization), and what is the most common headline penalty among those cases?
```

```
**Receipts:** The top name is the same as the one shown in the tool's output. I did not pull per-row receipts for this answer.

**Caveats:**
1. The data does not record which party excavated, so this is the count of cases naming that party as "other party", not a count of who was hit or who dug.
2. The second and third most common names, CenterPoint Energy Indiana (2,489, 34.4%) and Citizens Gas (923, 12.7%), are also utilities. That means utilities are the other party in most non-utility cases.
```

```
You are answering an actuarial question about Indiana's dig-damage enforcement record: the Indiana
Utility Regulatory Commission's orders approving the Underground Plant Protection Advisory Committee's
recommendations under Indiana Code chapter 8-1-26, for the 58 orders dated 2021-01-01 through 2025-12-31.
Definitions used in every question:
- **Case:** one recommendation form in an order (one case number on one page).
- **Year:** the year of the order's date, not the damage date, unless the question says otherwise.
- **Cite:** the IC 8-1-26 citation under "finds the following violation(s)". Every case has exactly one.
  **Base section:** the cite without "IC 8-1-26-" and without any parenthesized word. Example:
  "IC 8-1-26-16(h)(expire)" has base section "16(h)" and qualifier "expire". A parenthesized word is
  two or more letters (hyphens allowed); single letters or digits in parentheses, like (h) or (a)(2), are
  part of the base section, so "20(a)(3)(b)" is its own base section, not "20(a)(2)".
- **Headline penalty:** the first dollar amount in the committee's recommendation, read as printed but
  ignoring a doubled "$" and any space after a thousands comma ("$$1000" is $1,000; "$3, 500" is $3,500).
  A recommendation with no "$" amount has a headline penalty of $0.
- **Utility respondent:** a case whose respondent name, ignoring case and leading spaces, starts with one of:
  "CenterPoint", "Northern Indiana Public Service", "NIPSCO", "Citizens Gas", "Citizens Energy",
  "Vectren", "Indiana American Water", "Ohio Valley Gas", "American Electric Power", "Duke Energy",
  "Community Natural Gas", "Midwest Natural Gas", "Frontier Communications". Every other case is a
  **non-utility respondent**.
Give each number exactly as the question asks (rounding stated). If you cannot compute it from data you
have, say so and give your best estimate, labelled as an estimate.
```

```
- Tool: `python3 reference/tools/cases.py group --by other_party_norm --utility no --top 10` gives `northern indiana public service company n=3015 (41.63%)` out of 7,243 kept rows.
- Independent check in Python (csv module, the definition above): 7,243 non-utility rows; top name `northern indiana public service company` = 3,015 (41.6%). The runner-up is `centerpoint energy indiana` with 2,489 (34.4%).

**Receipts** (other_party = Northern Indiana Public Service Company, non-utility respondent):
- ord_PSD_011525.pdf p19, case 32551, IC 8-1-26-20(a)(2), respondent Bercot-Gibson Construction Co, Inc.
- ord_PSD_060822.pdf p8, case 25880, IC 8-1-26-16(h), respondent Eli Etter
- ord_PSD_122822.pdf p163, case 27443, IC 8-1-26-16(h), respondent Ruth Delorme

**Caveats:**
1. The data does not say which party dug. "Other party" is the second named party on the form, so this is a count of cases that name NIPSCO, not a count of who was hit.
2. The top names are utilities, and the form lists them as the other party on non-utility cases. Spelling variants of the same company are not merged, so the counts are floors.
```

```
**Reading taken:** A case is one row (one form page), as the desk's rules define it. - The 2025 orders are excluded from the 2023 and 2024 figures and from the 12.6% only through the blank-date row; the 12.6% covers all parseable years, 2021–2025.
```

```
- `python3 reference/tools/cases.py stat --field lag_days --year 2023` gives n = 2107, mean = 227.34.
- `python3 reference/tools/cases.py stat --field lag_days --year 2024` gives n = 1897, mean = 187.67.
- `python3 reference/tools/cases.py count --expr "r['lag_days']!='' and r['lag_days']>365"` gives 1259 of 9958 rows (12.64%). That tool percentage uses 9,958 as the denominator, which includes the unparseable row. Against the 9,957 parseable cases the figure is 12.6%.
- I recomputed the same figures independently from the raw CSV and got identical results.

**Other context:** Mean lag by order year is 378.9 (2021), 276.6 (2022), 227.3 (2023), 187.7 (2024) and 192.8 (2025). Lags range from 89 to 1,177 days. The 2021 and 2022 values are influenced by the order-year batching, so the downward trend should not be read as a change in how quickly damages were reported.
```

```
  part of the base section, so "20(a)(3)(b)" is its own base section, not "20(a)(2)".
- **Headline penalty:** the first dollar amount in the committee's recommendation, read as printed but
  ignoring a doubled "$" and any space after a thousands comma ("$$1000" is $1,000; "$3, 500" is $3,500).
  A recommendation with no "$" amount has a headline penalty of $0.
- **Utility respondent:** a case whose respondent name, ignoring case and leading spaces, starts with one of:
  "CenterPoint", "Northern Indiana Public Service", "NIPSCO", "Citizens Gas", "Citizens Energy",
  "Vectren", "Indiana American Water", "Ohio Valley Gas", "American Electric Power", "Duke Energy",
  "Community Natural Gas", "Midwest Natural Gas", "Frontier Communications". Every other case is a
```

```
  two or more letters (hyphens allowed); single letters or digits in parentheses, like (h) or (a)(2), are
  part of the base section, so "20(a)(3)(b)" is its own base section, not "20(a)(2)".
- **Headline penalty:** the first dollar amount in the committee's recommendation, read as printed but
  ignoring a doubled "$" and any space after a thousands comma ("$$1000" is $1,000; "$3, 500" is $3,500).
  A recommendation with no "$" amount has a headline penalty of $0.
- **Utility respondent:** a case whose respondent name, ignoring case and leading spaces, starts with one of:
  "CenterPoint", "Northern Indiana Public Service", "NIPSCO", "Citizens Gas", "Citizens Energy",
  "Vectren", "Indiana American Water", "Ohio Valley Gas", "American Electric Power", "Duke Energy",
  "Community Natural Gas", "Midwest Natural Gas", "Frontier Communications". Every other case is a
  **non-utility respondent**.
```

```
**Answer:**
- (a) Non-utility cases with headline penalty ≥ $5,000: **2023: 41.5%** (623 of 1,502); **2024: 48.3%** (687 of 1,421).
- (b) Utility-respondent share of all cases: **2023: 28.7%** (605 of 2,107); **2024: 25.1%** (476 of 1,897).

**Reading taken:** Years are order-date years. "At least $5,000" is inclusive (≥ 5000). Headline penalty, utility respondent and cite parsing follow the PREAMBLE definitions as implemented in the tool. $0 cases stay in every denominator.

**Commands and output** (run from `/tmp/r2run-3wr0h15v/folder`):

    python3 reference/tools/cases.py count --year 2023 --utility no
    -> 1502 of 9958 rows (15.08%)
    python3 reference/tools/cases.py count --year 2023 --utility no --penalty-min 5000
    -> 623 of 9958 rows (6.26%)     [623/1502 = 41.48%]
    python3 reference/tools/cases.py count --year 2024 --utility no
    -> 1421 of 9958 rows (14.27%)
    python3 reference/tools/cases.py count --year 2024 --utility no --penalty-min 5000
    -> 687 of 9958 rows (6.90%)     [687/1421 = 48.35%, shown as 48.3%]
    python3 reference/tools/cases.py count --year 2023
    -> 2107 of 9958 rows (21.16%)

    -> 605 of 9958 rows (6.08%)     [605/2107 = 28.71%]
    python3 reference/tools/cases.py count --year 2024
    -> 1897 of 9958 rows (19.05%)
    python3 reference/tools/cases.py count --year 2024 --utility yes
    -> 476 of 9958 rows (4.78%)     [476/1897 = 25.09%]

**Receipts:**
- 2023 ≥ $5,000, non-utility: ord_PSD_011823.pdf p3 case 27494; ord_PSD_122023.pdf p303 case 30206.

**Caveats:**
- Counts are cases (form rows) by order year, not dig damages. The data has no exposure measure, so these are shares of cases, not rates per dig.
- Many headline $5,000 amounts come from "Training or $5,000 if respondent fails to complete training" wording, where training avoids the fine. The question uses the headline as defined, so these are included. Excluding training wording would change (a) substantially.
```
